- Decision, receivers: `HttpReceiver`, `SmtpReceiver` and `WebSocketReceiver` started inside a test are stopped after its teardown (`after_this_case`), so a failed assertion no longer leaves one listening; one started outside a test is left alone.
- Reason: the Rails assertions and helpers a ported test calls, each over what the framework already writes (rows, responses, deliveries), so production code gains no test switch.
- Spinel: one return type per name (rules 10, 34, 41), hence `assert_raises` answering the message, the block assertions answering nil, the reader's prefixed `HtmlNode` readers and the helper tests' `render_helper_template`; Ruby's own exception classes answer no `superclass` (spikes/NOTES.md rule 104), so the chain above them is read from a table of their parents; lambdas passed positionally (rules 42, 82); yields into a local (rule 90); byte scanners through a fresh local (rule 62); no `assert_predicate` (a computed `send`, rule 1).
- Rejected: Nokogiri or a repairing HTML5 parser (a C library, and a repaired tree would let malformed templates pass); recording broadcasts in a test-only hook in `Stream`; refusing `:test` delivery outside the test environment (it would break a staging smoke setup); fakes that replace a class's `new` (no run-time redefinition; the receivers stay, D32); returning the exception from `assert_raises` (two return types for one name).
- Consequences: a Rails test that reads `assert_raises(...).message` changes to the String; HTML a template writes without closing its tags fails `assert_select`; the mail setting is not reset between tests, so a test of the SMTP path puts it back.
- Verification: `test/assertions.rb`, `test_html.rb` (the reader and `assert_select`), `test_broadcasts.rb`, `test_integration.rb`, `test_mail_jobs.rb`, `job_helpers.rb`, `receivers.rb`, the `cli_*` generator tests; `script/test-command-e2e`, `script/generators-e2e`, `script/auth-e2e`; examples/blog's component, broadcast and mail tests.
- Amended 2026-11-13 (after the final review): `cookies` (and `Client#cookies`) is a `Cybertrain::Test::ClientCookies` rather than a Hash (`[]`, `[]=`, `key?`, `delete`, `keys`, `size`, `empty?`, `to_h`; a Symbol reads the cookie of that name); a cookie past its `Max-Age` or `Expires` on the clock, so also after `travel_to`, is gone (`Max-Age` wins), and one written with `[]=` never expires. `follow_redirect!` after a response that is not a redirect fails the test (`follow_redirect!: the last response was <status>, not a redirect (3xx with a Location)`) and never sends the Location's fragment. `with_forgery_protection` no longer re-applies the configuration, so a test's upload root, zone, locale and log level are kept. `assert_select` takes a Range as its count (refused beside `count:` / `minimum:` / `maximum:`, and when negative or crossed) and refuses a backslash (`CSS escapes (\) are not supported`) and a Range without both ends (`a Range count needs both ends (got 2..)`); a binary body is read as UTF-8. The cookie jar ignores an unreadable `Max-Age` and expires a negative one; it has no `each`, `length`, `include?`, `fetch` or `==`. `Cookies.serialize`'s `expires:` defaults to `Cookies::NO_EXPIRES`, not nil (spikes/NOTES.md rule 109). The generated `SessionTestHelper` also has `authenticity_token_in(body)`. Still not offered: `session`, `flash`, `assigns`, `host!` on IntegrationTest.

### D85. Optional subsystems: an application compiles what it requires (2026-11-13)

- Context: Spinel's whole-program analysis is most of every build (44 of the 53 s of a controller edit on the 4-core VM of docs/performance-next.md, "The development flow"), it grows faster than the program (an exponent of about 1.7, docs/spinel-feedback.md "Build time"), and Spinel compiles every method a required file defines, called or not. A scaffolded application required all of `cybertrain`, so it compiled jobs, the scheduler, Turbo Streams, WebSocket feeds, the mailer, the cache and rate limiting whether it used them or not. A spike (a throwaway copy of a915504 with the subsystems cut out by hand) measured 2,690 against 2,176 methods, 41.6 s against 30-31 s of analysis and 89.6 s against 68.7 s for a full build, and showed that what costs is what is wired into reachable code (the template helper dispatch, the middleware stack, the server role's boot), not what is merely defined.
- Decision: the application says what it uses, in `bin/NAME.rb`, the way Rails' `config/application.rb` lists railties. `require "cybertrain/core"` is HTTP, routing, controllers, models, views and helpers, assets, sessions, CSRF, CSP, the console, `db` and `migrate`; `cybertrain/jobs` (with the scheduler, the worker role, the jobs page and the `jobs` command), `cybertrain/streams`, `cybertrain/feeds` (loaded by `cybertrain/websocket`), `cybertrain/mailer`, `cybertrain/mailer/later` (with mailer and jobs), `cybertrain/cache` and `cybertrain/rate_limit` (with the cache) each add one subsystem, on a line after `require "cybertrain/core"`: `cybertrain/jobs` and `cybertrain/feeds` (so `mailer/later` and `websocket` too) load the core themselves, `cybertrain/streams`, `cybertrain/mailer`, `cybertrain/cache` and `cybertrain/rate_limit` do not (the mailer must load under CRuby and without SQLite). `require "cybertrain"` is all of them, unchanged. `turbo_frame_tag`, `dom_id`, `render turbo_stream:` and `format.turbo_stream` stay in the core. `cybertrain new` writes the core alone in `bin/NAME.rb` and `bin/db.rb` (which loads `config/app.rb`, so it needs the same lines); `generate job` adds `cybertrain/jobs` and `generate authentication` its four lines (at first `cybertrain/rate_limit` alone; see the phase 4b paragraph below) to both, after the last `require "cybertrain..."` line, unless the line or `require "cybertrain"` is there.
- The mechanism: the core must never name a subsystem's class, or naming it pulls the class into the program (`script/check-core-closure` keeps the core's closure free of them). `Cybertrain::Feature` is a base class in the core whose hooks do nothing and return a fixed type: boot problems, start, stop and the TERM flag of the server role, `failed?` for a role waiting on a stop, the `/up` health line, the stream endpoint's position in the middleware stack, `Main.run`'s commands and their help rows, the template helper dispatch, and periodic work for the scheduler. A subsystem's entry file defines a subclass, registers one instance at require time (a second require is a no-op) and reopens core classes for its own methods (`Controller#turbo_stream`, `Controller.rate_limit`). The features are ranked (`feature_rank`), so the server role starts and stops `Job`, `Scheduler`, `Background` and `Stream::Poller` in the order it did before, whatever the order of the require lines. No block is stored on a feature (spikes/NOTES.md rule 88): a scheduler task calls a module method with the feature's index. `Cybertrain::FeatureCatalog` is the one table of each line's helpers, roles, commands and what it loads besides, read by gen and by the binary's messages.
- What a missing line does: Ruby code naming the subsystem does not compile (the error names the constant or method; the guide's table names the line); a view calling a subsystem's helper stops `spin run gen` and `--check` with `PATH:LINE: HELPER needs require "LINE" in bin/NAME.rb` (gen reads `bin/NAME.rb` lexically for its require lines), and a template rendered anyway raises `undefined helper`; `./NAME worker`, `jobs` and `feeds` print the line they need and exit 1 instead of "unknown command"; `/up` reports only on loaded subsystems.
- The cache without jobs prunes itself: the first write of a process and the first after each `cache_prune_interval` delete at most 1000 expired rows (the scheduled task's statement budget), so a busy cache never grows past what the scheduled prune allowed. With jobs loaded it is the scheduled task `"cybertrain cache prune"` as before.
- The measurement on the real implementation (fabd28a, docs/performance-next.md "Optional subsystems"): the same scaffolded application with `cybertrain/core` against `cybertrain`, 2,205 against 2,767 methods, 38.4 and 38.9 s against 52.6 and 52.1 s of analysis, 83.4 s against 108.3 s for a build from an empty object cache, about 47 s against about 65 s for a controller edit with the dev loop's incremental build, 60.0 s against 84.0 s for `db status` after a controller edit. The binary served the scaffold's pages and `/up` with 200.
- Rejected: gen detecting the subsystems from the application's code and writing the requires itself (a lexical scan of Ruby and of `config/app.rb` would be wrong in both directions, a silent miss is a compile error far from its cause, and the explicit line is what a Rails user expects to read); making `require "cybertrain"` the core alone (it would break every existing application and examples/blog on upgrade, for a speed-up they can take by editing one line); rate limiting falling back to an in-memory counter without the cache (sign-in throttling would then count per process, a security property changing silently with a require line; it loads the cache instead); the mailer always loading jobs (sending now needs no database and no job thread; `deliver_later` is its own line, `cybertrain/mailer/later`).
- Known gaps: a `jobs_page` drawn in `config/routes.rb` without `cybertrain/jobs` is a compile error of the generated controller, not gen's message; a job whose `retry_on` or `discard_on` names a `Cybertrain::Mailer` error makes `gen/app.rb` require `cybertrain/mailer` itself (so the mailer is compiled in without a line in `bin/NAME.rb`). Since phase 5a the generators write tests on `test/test_helper.rb`, and `cybertrain test` compiles them with `require "cybertrain"` (below).
- Compatibility: none broken. `require "cybertrain"` loads everything and an application that has it builds and behaves as before; the speed-up is opt-in, by replacing that line with the core and the lines the application uses. Not in this change: a smaller core (sessions, CSP, the console, `db` in the binary), a `bin/db.rb` that requires only the migrator, and moving examples/blog to the explicit list.
- With phase 4b (D79, D80, merged after this was written): i18n (`cybertrain/i18n`, the bundled dictionaries) and `Cybertrain::Token` are core, required by `cybertrain/core`; the bundled dictionary keeps the jobs page's strings (data, which `script/check-core-closure` reads as text in `cybertrain/locales/bundled.rb` only). `Cybertrain::MailMessage` is the mailer's: `cybertrain/mailer` loads it with `#deliver_now`, and `cybertrain/mailer/later` reopens it to add `#deliver_later`. `cybertrain generate authentication` writes code that needs four lines (the jobs page it draws and the mail job's thread, the mailers and `User`'s `Mailer.address_problem`, `deliver_later`, `rate_limit`), so it adds `cybertrain/jobs`, `cybertrain/mailer`, `cybertrain/mailer/later` and `cybertrain/rate_limit` in every mode and on the upgrade path (a 0.4 application's `require "cybertrain"` already loads them).
- With phase 5a (D81 to D84, merged after this was written; this entry was D81 until then): `Cybertrain::Clock`, `ZonedTime` and the durations are core (`cybertrain/core` requires `cybertrain/clock`, `zoned_time` and `durations`), and the cache's self-prune reads `Cache.now`, which is the clock (its `Cache.clock=` seam is gone). The test framework is not the runtime core and is not cut by these lines: the program `cybertrain test` compiles (gen/tests.rb, D83) requires `cybertrain`, every subsystem, whatever `bin/NAME.rb` requires, so an application on `cybertrain/core` runs its tests unchanged (its test program is not smaller, and code that names a subsystem without its line fails at `spin build`, not in the tests). `cybertrain/test_help`, which the generated `test/test_helper.rb` requires, loads every test helper; those that need a subsystem live in that subsystem's test file, which `script/check-core-closure` lists with it: `perform_enqueued_jobs` and the job assertions in `cybertrain/test/jobs`, `assert_emails` and `assert_no_emails` (over `Mailer.deliveries`) in `cybertrain/test/mail`, `assert_broadcasts` and `assert_turbo_stream` in `cybertrain/test/broadcasts`. `cybertrain/test`, which a framework test on `cybertrain/core` requires, names none. Making the test program mirror `bin/NAME.rb` (and `cybertrain/test_help` load each helper only with its subsystem) is left for later: a require cannot depend on another at run time, so gen would have to write the helper lines too. `MailMessage` keeps main's file names: `cybertrain/mailer/mail_message` (the fields and `#deliver_now`, loaded by `cybertrain/mailer`, whose `Mailer.deliveries` holds them) and `cybertrain/mailer/later` (`#deliver_later`); `cybertrain/mail_message` loads both. `./NAME jobs` applies the configured zone as `console` does, in `cybertrain/jobs`. The boot warning about `mail_delivery_method = :test` outside tests stays core, a `Config` check that names no class.
- Verification, compiled: `test/features_core.rb` (the core alone serves a page), `test/features_jobs.rb`, `features_streams.rb`, `features_feeds.rb`, `features_mailer.rb` and `features_rate_limit.rb` (each line alone), `test/features_order.rb` (the rank, whatever the require order), `test/gen_view_requires.rb` (gen's refusal and acceptance), `test/cli_new.rb`, `cli_generate_job.rb` and `cli_authentication.rb` (the lines written once), `test/cache_self_prune.rb` (the clock moved with `travel_to`); the existing suite requires `cybertrain` and passes unchanged. The spec is docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-13-optional-subsystems-design.md.

### D87. A CRuby preview in the development loop: `cybertrain server` answers from CRuby at once and hands the port to the compiled binary (2026-11-12, withdraws the rejection of running on CRuby in development)

- Context: under D12 an edit to one Ruby file is not served until gen, Spinel's analysis of the whole program and the C compiler have run. That was about 8 s on examples/blog on 2026-11-05 (gen 3.3 s, Spinel's analysis 3.9 s, the C compiler and linker 0.75 s; docs/performance-next.md), and 65.6 to 85.5 s for an edit or a route on the 4-core VM of docs/performance-next.md's development-flow table (2026-11-12), where Spinel's analysis alone is 44 s of every edit and grows with the framework. Three records said no to running the application on CRuby: principle 5 ("Keep application execution and generation on Spinel"), section 8 ("running on CRuby" among what was left out) and docs/performance-next.md ("An interpreted development mode on CRuby: it hides the Spinel differences a developer most needs to see, and needs a shim for every `ffi_func`"). On 2026-11-12 the maintainer withdrew the restriction for development: the development experience was too bad to keep. Production is untouched. The design is [the spec](superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-cruby-preview-design.md), the plan's decisions P1 to P29 are in [the plan](superpowers/plans/2026-11-12-cruby-preview-plan.md).
- Decision: `cybertrain server` (the gem CLI, under CRuby) is a supervisor. It owns the listening socket for its whole life and runs one child on it at a time: the application under CRuby right after a change, the compiled binary once a background build has made it. The page says which one rendered it.
  - Startup: the pinned Spinel (`Toolchain.ensure!`), the load path (the `-I` directories of `spin flags`), the framework's dev files (`cybertrain/dev/supervision.rb`, `state_file.rb`, `watcher.rb`, `watched.rb`) loaded from the application's framework, never from the gem, which ships none of them (P21), so the supervisor and its children share one definition of the state file and of the environment a child reads; the leftovers of a supervisor that was killed; the address (`bin/NAME.rb dev-address` under CRuby; a port argument overrides the port); the bind; the first round.
  - A round (a change to `Dev::WATCHED` minus `Dev::IGNORED`, views excluded as in D12, or the start): cancel the running build, run gen under CRuby (`ruby -E UTF-8 -I<dirs> bin/gen.rb`, which writes the same bytes as the compiled gen; the gate checks it on examples/blog), stop the current child, start the CRuby child (`ruby -E UTF-8 -I<dirs> -r cybertrain/preview bin/NAME.rb PORT`), start the build. Changes that arrive meanwhile coalesce into one more round.
  - The build, logged to `tmp/rebuild.log`: the session's first build is plain `spin build NAME` when the binary is newer than every `*.rb` of the application and of the other `-I` directories (P23), so spin's own check finds an unchanged application up to date and the binary serves at once (on a copy of examples/blog, 4 cores, 2026-11-13, the log said `=> spinel: ready in 0.3s; serving the compiled binary`); every later build, and a first one on a stale binary, is `spin build NAME -- --incremental` (P24). When a build has written the binary (whether it then succeeded, failed or was cancelled), the supervisor sets the binary's modification time back to that build's launch time (the time of the `build.pid` it wrote at the launch), before the binary is started, so a save made during a build is always newer than the binary, in this session and in the next one, whose first build then compiles it (P25); a build left by a killed supervisor is set back the same way by the next start. A Spinel that refuses `--incremental` gets the plain command, as in D12. A build of an older generation is discarded.
  - The handover is stop, then start: TERM to the old child, which drains (D14), then the new child on the same socket. The socket stays open in the supervisor, so a connection made in between waits in the kernel's queue and is answered by the new child, and a connection the old child accepted just before its stop is answered by it, not reset (D14's first-request grace). A child that has not exited 10 s after TERM is killed.
  - A failed build leaves the CRuby child serving and shows D12's banner with the compiler's output on its HTML responses; neither loop puts the banner into a download (`Content-Disposition: attachment`) or a body the application encoded (`Content-Encoding`), the rule the indicator follows too. A binary that does not become ready, or exits while serving, is a difference only Spinel has: the CRuby child serves again, the page says "Spinel binary failed" with what the binary reported, and nothing retries it before the next change. When gen fails or the CRuby child cannot boot or stops (a SyntaxError arrives in a fraction of a second), the supervisor holds: it answers every request itself with a 503 page that shows the error and what it waits for (`Retry-After: 1`, a refresh every 2 s), and the next change leaves the hold. After a gen failure no build runs; when only the CRuby child failed, the build of that round goes on and takes the port if it succeeds. While `cybertrain build` holds `tmp/cybertrain-build.lock` there is no gen and no restart; the pending change is handled once the lock is gone.
  - Children read five supervision variables (`CYBERTRAIN_DEV_LISTEN_FD`, `_READY_FILE`, `_STATE_FILE`, `_FAILURE_FILE`, `_GENERATION`; `Dev::Supervision`), in development only. The supervisor's files are under `tmp/cybertrain-dev/`: `state` (generation, what serves, the build's state, a failure's output; the only channel from the supervisor to a child), `ready`, `failure`, `supervisor.pid`, `child.pid`, `build.pid`, and `ffi/`, the shim's compiled libraries. One supervisor per application: a second is refused while the first runs; a leftover child or build of a supervisor that was killed is stopped before the bind. INT, TERM and HUP stop the build and the child, remove the files and close the socket.
  - The FFI shim, `cybertrain/preview/` in the framework (never required by `cybertrain.rb`, so Spinel never compiles it), implements on `Module` the DSL Spinel provides natively (`ffi_lib`, `ffi_source`, `ffi_func`, `ffi_const`, `ffi_read_ptr`, `malloc`, `free`) through Fiddle. A module's `ffi_source` C is compiled at the first call of one of its functions (`cc -shared -fPIC`, linked against its `ffi_lib`) and cached in `tmp/cybertrain-dev/ffi/<sha256>.so`; the same C runs on both runtimes, and no FFI file of the framework was rewritten.
  - The framework, compiled and tested under Spinel: a supervised development server listens on the inherited descriptor (where the kernel does not answer `SO_ACCEPTCONN`, it is recognised as a listener by being bound with no peer: macOS's kernel source has no such option, a reading that only CI's compiled tests on macOS confirm, if and when they pass), builds no watcher or rebuilder, creates the readiness file, and `Roles.fail_boot` writes its message to the failure file; `Dev::ErrorPage` reads the build's state through `Dev::BuildStatus`, filled from the state file; `Dev::Indicator`, installed only under the supervisor, sets `X-Cybertrain-Runtime: cruby` or `spinel` on every response of the application (not on the supervisor's own 503) and writes one line in a corner of HTML pages (not into attachments or bodies the application encoded), kept current by a script it serves from a reserved development path (with the CSP nonce when the application uses nonces; styled through the CSSOM). The line's seven texts, owned by `Dev::Indicator.text` (start there to change one): `CRuby preview · Spinel building…`, `Spinel ready — reload`, `CRuby preview · Spinel build failed`, `CRuby preview · Spinel binary failed`, `Spinel`, `Source changed — reload`, and `CRuby preview` while nothing else applies. The page never reloads itself. Nothing of this is read by a production boot, and the old loop's output is unchanged down to its snapshots.
  - `cybertrain server --spinel` is D12's loop, unchanged, as are `spin run NAME` typed directly and the native CLI that `spin install` builds. When the preview cannot run, `cybertrain server` prints one line, `preview unavailable: <cause>; running the compiled loop`, and runs that loop. The causes, checked in this order: `CYBERTRAIN_ENV` is set to something other than `development` (a child outside development is not supervised and would bind the supervisor's port); the application's framework has no `cybertrain/preview.rb` (a release older than the preview), or no `cybertrain/dev/supervision.rb`; there is no `ruby` on `PATH`; the `ruby` on `PATH` cannot be started; it did not answer within 10 s; it cannot load `fiddle`. `cybertrain doctor` has a `fiddle` line.
- Measured: on the development container (4 cores, 2026-11-13, six runs of `script/preview-e2e` on a copy of examples/blog), step 3 (one model edit) is answered by CRuby 0.6 to 0.7 s after the save, and the binary of its incremental build (step 4) takes over about a minute after it (54 to 68 s); on the playground image, 0.6 s and 74 s on 4 CPUs (playground/README.md).
- What the preview does not show, stated in the guide (docs/guide/how-it-works.md, "The development loop"):
  - Whether the code compiles. The background build says so when it ends.
  - The runtime differences recorded in spikes/NOTES.md (exception ancestry, String encodings on sockets, `Float#round` on a narrowed Float, and the rest). They appear once the binary serves. The framework tests whose CRuby output differs from the compiled snapshots are listed with their causes in [test/cruby/parity-gaps.txt](../test/cruby/parity-gaps.txt).
  - Timing and concurrency: CRuby has a GVL and OS threads; `SPINEL_WORKERS` means nothing to it.
  - Background work (jobs, the scheduler, the stream poller, WebSocket feeds) runs in whichever child is serving, as it does in the binary; each edit restarts it twice (into CRuby, then into the binary) instead of once, and SSE and WebSocket clients reconnect twice. The same services run in both runtimes, for fidelity.
- A CRuby pass is not acceptance, for the preview as for the commit gate: the compiled binary and `spin test` decide (CONTRIBUTING.md; spikes/NOTES.md, "What CRuby cannot tell you").
- Rejected:
  - Always serving from CRuby, the Spinel build running only as a check whose failure shows as the banner (the author's first recommendation): instant, and compile errors still surface, but the runtime differences are never served; confirmation on the real runtime should arrive without asking for it.
  - A separate `cybertrain preview` command, CRuby only, with `cybertrain server` unchanged: the smallest change, but nothing tells the developer that the code does not compile until they run the other command.
  - An adapter over the `sqlite3` gem instead of the Fiddle shim: a gem dependency, and a second database path that only CRuby runs. The shim sits below the framework's own FFI files, so both runtimes run the same database code.
  - Reloading in-process by removing constants: fragile, and a restart costs about 0.4 s.
  - Starting the new child before stopping the old one: overlapping children would both run the job threads, the scheduler, the stream poller and the WebSocket feeds, and a feed would hold two connections to a third party. Stop-then-start refuses nothing, because the supervisor keeps the socket.
  - Reloading the page by itself when the binary takes over: it loses a form being filled in. The indicator says `Spinel ready — reload` instead.
  - Answering from both runtimes and comparing the responses: twice the side effects.
- Consequences: an edit is served in about a second (the aim is under one; since phase 5a gen also regenerates the application's tests, which adds about 0.4 s on examples/blog, and `script/preview-e2e` bounds it at 1.5 s), and the developer still ends on the compiled binary without asking. The development loop needs a Ruby that loads `fiddle` (doctor checks it; without one the compiled loop runs) and the C compiler already required. The framework carries `cybertrain/preview/` and the supervision code in `cybertrain/dev/`, all of it development-only. Background work and streaming clients restart twice per edit. The gap between the runtimes is a checked list rather than a belief: `script/test-cruby` runs the preview's own CRuby-only tests (`test/cruby/`), `script/preview-parity` (the `cruby-preview` CI job) runs every framework test under CRuby with the shim against the compiled snapshots, and `script/preview-e2e` (the `preview-e2e` CI job, on Ubuntu) drives the supervisor on a copy of examples/blog.
- Known limits and open questions:
  - On macOS only the CRuby-only tests run (`script/test-cruby`, the macOS leg of the `cruby-preview` CI job, since 2026-11-13): they run the identity rules without `/proc` (`ps -ww`, the joined argument line), clang's `-shared` and the `.dylib` lookup against stand-in processes and the shim's own probe library. The supervisor on a real application (`script/preview-e2e`, which reads `/proc`) and `script/preview-parity` (GNU `timeout`) run on Linux only, so the preview on macOS beyond those tests is unverified.
  - `cybertrain build` holds `tmp/cybertrain-build.lock` with an exclusive `flock` for its whole run (2026-11-14, from saeki-mototsune/CyberTrain#233), and the supervisor asks `CLI::BuildLock#held?` (a shared `flock` for an instant), never reading the PID or the file's age and never removing the file. The lock is still read before and after the blocking steps (P17), and a running supervisor build is not cancelled when the lock appears.
  - (Closed 2026-11-13, P26.) The shim loads a library from `tmp/cybertrain-dev/ffi/` only when the directory and the file are owned by the process's effective user and neither is writable by its group or others (`File.stat`: `uid == Process.euid`, `mode & 0o022 == 0`). It creates a missing directory 0700 and writes a library 0700. A directory that fails is an error naming it and what is wrong (the child fails to boot, the supervisor's 503 shows it); nothing is compiled elsewhere instead, and an existing directory's mode is never changed. A file that fails is removed and compiled once more. What is not checked: the directories above `ffi/` (a directory put in its place by someone who can write to `tmp/cybertrain-dev/` is theirs, and fails the owner check), and the libraries named by `ffi_lib` (`libsqlite3`, `libz`), which the dynamic loader finds as for any program.
  - The supervisor sets `build/bin/NAME`'s modification time back to its build's launch (P25): another tool reading that time sees the launch, not the write.
  - The gem's supervisor subclasses the application framework's `Dev::Watcher` and relies on its private methods and instance variables (named in `cybertrain/dev/watcher.rb`), a contract across two releases.
  - Conditional requests are validated against the child as well as the application (P6): the application's `ETag` leaves with `-dev-<runtime>-<generation>` inside its quotes. On GET and HEAD, `If-None-Match` reaches the application, the suffix removed, only when every tag carries this child's suffix (it is dropped otherwise), and `If-Modified-Since` never does, so a page validated by `Last-Modified` alone is answered 200 in the preview. On every method `If-Match`, and on the others `If-None-Match`, reach the application with this child's suffix removed from each tag (weak prefixes, lists and `*` as sent; since 2026-11-13, P29): a PUT or PATCH that sends back the tag it was given matches as it would without the preview. A tag of another runtime or generation keeps its suffix and does not match, so an `If-Match` from a page rendered before the last change or handover is answered 412 in the preview, where the compiled loop, whose tags do not change with a restart, may accept it; `If-Unmodified-Since` reaches the application as sent.
- Verification: compiled, `test/dev_supervision.rb`, `test/dev_supervised_boot.rb`, `test/dev_state_file.rb`, `test/dev_error_page.rb`, `test/dev_indicator.rb`, `test/dev_application.rb` and `test/cli_toolchain.rb` (doctor's `fiddle` line); under CRuby, `test/cruby/dev_server_machine.rb`, `dev_server_processes.rb`, `dev_server_holding.rb`, `dev_server.rb` (startup and the fallbacks), `preview_ffi.rb` and `preview_failure.rb` (`script/test-cruby`), `script/preview-parity`, and `script/preview-e2e` (the `preview-e2e` CI job, on Ubuntu, since 2026-11-13: start, handover, edits, a build lock, a killed supervisor, a SyntaxError, a compile-only error, Ctrl-C, no request refused or reset across the handovers, the indicator and its nonce, the reuse of an up-to-date binary); the gate's "gen under CRuby leaves examples/blog unchanged"; the playground's smoke test.

## 5. Request flow

1. `Server` parses HTTP/1.1 on the accepted socket and builds a `Request`.
2. Middleware chain: logging -> static files -> `_method` override -> session (decoding the signed cookie; decrypting the encrypted one since 2026-11-11, D61) -> CSRF verification -> router. Since 2026-11-11 the host check and the https redirect come first when configured, and the Content Security Policy header is written on the way out (D61).
3. The router walks the route table in `gen/routes.rb` and calls the dispatch clause of the matching route: `PostsController.new(ctx).process(:show) { |c| c.show }`.
4. `process` runs the `before_action`s in order (the symbol form via the `case` in the generated `run_callback(name)`, the block form via `block.call(self)` with the controller as the argument; the chain stops as soon as one renders or redirects), calls the action body, implicitly renders the template named after the action if neither a render nor a redirect happened, and finally runs the `after_action`s.
5. render passes `view_assigns` (generated), the locals and the helpers to the template engine, renders in an environment of plain polymorphic values, and wraps the result in the layout.
6. The `Response` is written back to the socket. With keep-alive, it waits for the next request.

## 6. Generator inputs and outputs

| Input | Output | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `db/schema.rb` | `gen/models/<model>.rb` | `attr_accessor`, the `case` for `read_attribute` / `write_attribute` / `assign_attributes`, `from_row`, `to_json`, `attribute_names`, associations derived from foreign keys, `<Model>Relation` |
| `config/routes.rb` | `gen/routes.rb` | the route table, the dispatch `case`, `*_path` / `*_url`, the polymorphic path `case` |
| `app/controllers/**/*.rb` (lexical scan) | `gen/controllers.rb` | per-controller `view_assigns` and the `run_callback(name)` `case` (open class) |
| `app/models/**/*.rb` (lexical scan) | appended to `gen/models/<model>.rb` | per-model `call_view_method(name)` `case` (argument-less `def` only), and its names in `attribute_or_method?` (D71) |
| `app/controllers/concerns/**`, `app/models/concerns/**` (lexical scan) | `gen/concerns.rb`, `gen/concerns/**` | the concerns' `included` blocks spliced into each including class, `class_methods` forwarders, and the concern modules with those blocks blanked (2026-11-11, D60) |
| `app/assets/**`, `app/javascript/**`, `vendor/javascript/**`, `config/importmap.rb` | `gen/assets.rb`, `public/assets/` | the logical-to-digested `case`, the import map and its preloads; the digested files and `.manifest` (2026-11-11, D63) |
| `config/locales/**/*.yml` | `gen/locales.rb` | the application's dictionary: two Hashes filled by methods of at most 50 keys, `lookup` / `lookup_list`, installed into `I18n` when the file loads (2026-11-12, D79) |
| file list of `app/` | `gen/app.rb` | list of `require_relative` |

All generated code is committed. CI runs `spin run gen` and then `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all -- gen/`, which must print nothing: a changed file, a file that is gone and a file never committed all fail it (`git diff --exit-code` did not see the last, issue #205).

The inputs above are what gen reads when it runs. What it is compiled from is a different set: whatever `bin/gen.rb` reaches through its requires, which `config/routes.rb` can extend with `app/routes/extra.rb` or a package called through `Package.draw(r)`.

The development rebuild (D12) uses that difference. spin recompiles an executable when any Ruby file of the package or of a path dependency changes, so an edit to a controller used to spend about 3 s of an 8 s rebuild compiling a generator identical to the one on disk. So `Dev::Rebuilder` first asks the compiler what gen is now: `spinel bin/gen.rb -c -o build/gen-check.c --force --no-line-map $(spin flags)`, quietly, about 0.3 s on `examples/blog`. If that succeeds, `build/bin/gen` exists and the C is byte-identical to `build/gen-compiled.c`, the C saved from the last successful full rebuild, the rebuild runs `./build/bin/gen` instead of `spin run gen`. The rebuild always runs gen, because a controller edit can change `gen/controllers.rb`.

The check compares the compiler's output because only the compiler knows what is compiled in. An earlier version followed `require`s by regex and signed the files it found, and disagreed with the compiler on `require File.join(__dir__, "..", "app", "routes", "x")`, which is folded and compiled but was not followed: the stale gen then wrote wrong `gen/` output with no error. Comparing C needs no list of inputs, so there is none to get wrong. `--no-line-map` drops the `#line` directives, so a comment moved in a required file costs no compile; the 0.3 s is paid on every rebuild, and it is what buys back the 3 s. The two files live in `build/` because spin warns on every command about spinel's output anywhere else in the package, and because they should go when `build/bin/gen` goes.

Any failure of the check (no `spinel` on `PATH`, `spin flags` failing, a compile error, an unreadable file) means not fresh, and the full `spin run gen` reports what is really wrong. The check runs before the build, so an edit made during the build reads as a change next time. After a successful full run the check runs again, and if the compiler still gives the same C it is renamed to the saved one and `build/gen-compiled.stamp` records the binary's modification time and size; a different C means an edit during the build may be in the binary, so nothing is saved. A direct run records nothing, nor does a failed run unless the check was fresh, when the binary came from the saved C unless such an edit says otherwise. The stamp is part of fresh: `spin run gen` by hand from edited sources, then the edit undone, leaves the C matching and a different binary. When the C matches and the stamp does not, the rebuild deletes the binary before `spin run gen`, because spin reuses a binary newer than its sources. A stale check deletes the saved C right away, because `spin run gen` replaces the binary even if the build after it fails, and undoing the edit must then not read as a match. A failed direct run is followed at once by the full command, so a broken binary never strands the loop; the price is that a compile error in the application is built twice.

What the check does not see is whatever reaches gen's binary without changing its C: a `.c` file of a path dependency, which spin compiles but gen's output does not depend on unless a generator calls into it, and the Spinel runtime, which the C only `#include`s.

## 7. Historical template-language draft (2026-09-24)

This section preserves the MVP draft, not the current language contract. Use [the template-language guide](guide/template-language.md) for supported syntax and D38/D62 for later decisions.

ERB tags: `<% %>`, `<%= %>` (escaped), `<%== %>` (unescaped), `<%# %>`, `<%- -%>` (trim), and a leading `<%# locals: (a:, b:) %>`.

Expressions:

- Literals: integers, floats, strings (`"..."` allows `#{}` interpolation, `'...'` does not), symbols, `nil` / `true` / `false`, arrays `[...]`, hashes `{ key: value }` (the same form as keyword arguments)
- Variables: `@ivar` (from `view_assigns`), locals (`each` bindings and strict locals)
- Calls: `recv.method`, `recv.method(args, key: value)`, `recv&.method`, `method(args)` (helpers)
- Operators: `== != < <= > >= && || ! + - * / % ?:`
- Control: `if` / `elsif` / `else` / `unless` / `end`, `each do |x|` / `each_with_index do |x, i|`, `form_with(...) do |f|`
- Per-value table of allowed methods: String (`upcase` `downcase` `capitalize` `strip` `size` `length` `empty?` `to_s`), Integer / Float (arithmetic and `to_s`), Time (`strftime` `year` `month` `day` `to_s`), Array (`size` `length` `empty?` `first` `last` `any?`), Model (generated attributes, generated associations, `errors`, `persisted?`, `new_record?`, `to_param`, those declared with `view_methods`), `errors` (`any?` `full_messages` `[]` `count`)

Explicitly unsupported: arbitrary blocks (`map { }` and so on), local definitions other than assignment, `case`, regular expressions, ranges, method definitions, `require`. (This is the draft of 2026-09-24. Since 2026-11-05 `case` / `when`, blocks on a fixed list of Array and Hash methods and the `tag` builder exist (D9, D38); docs/guide/template-language.md describes what is built.)

## 8. What was deliberately left out

- `rails console` as a Ruby prompt (`eval` does not exist). Since 2026-11-05 `./<app> console` evaluates the template expression language over the generated models, and `./<app> dbconsole` opens `sqlite3` (D41)
- Constants and arbitrary Ruby in templates (`case`, blocks on a fixed list of methods and the `tag` builder exist since 2026-11-05, D9), compile-time detection of type errors in templates, error pages with stack traces (still out after phase 5a: a release build has no backtrace, spike item 5)
- A fully self-contained single-file distribution. Production views are already embedded in `gen/views.rb`; public assets, native libraries and persistent storage remain external. Embedding views does not require a compiled-template backend.
- `has_many :through`, polymorphic associations, nested `includes` (`namespace`, formats and `respond_to` arrived on 2026-11-11 in D58 and D59, with concerns, the controller and view additions and assets in D60 to D63) (i18n arrived on 2026-11-12 in D79, and password reset, confirmation, lockout, remember me, account pages and admin users in D80) (authentication arrived on 2026-10-22 in the form of D15: code written by the generator, plus `Cybertrain::Password`) (the `transaction` API in D20, mailers in D27 and jobs in D28 arrived on 2026-10-25 in minimal form) (the outbound WebSocket client arrived on 2026-10-26 in D33, and browser delivery over Server-Sent Events and WebSocket on 2026-11-05 in D36 and D37) (one level of `includes` arrived on 2026-11-07 in D47) (Rails-shaped test classes, fixtures, time travel and the time helpers arrived on 2026-11-12 in D81 to D84; transactional tests, `skip`, `parallelize`, system tests and TimeWithZone stay out). Several of these are later phases of the programme in D1
- Rack compatibility, running Rails code unchanged (porting by hand is in scope since 2026-11-05, D1), running on CRuby in production (in development `cybertrain server` serves a CRuby preview until the compiled binary takes over, D87)

## 9. Unverified assumptions and spike plan (M0)

Small Spinel programs are placed in `spikes/` and verified against `2026.09.12`. (The results are in `spikes/NOTES.md`. The programs themselves were deleted after verification.)

| # | What is verified | If it fails |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Installing `spinel` / `spin`, the optimisation levels in `spinel --help`, compile time at the scale of 2,000 lines | Re-evaluate how the development loop (D12) feels |
| 2 | Whether a non-literal `send` can dispatch to literal names, the return type, the block form of `instance_exec` | **Result**: `send` works, but the 128-literal limit makes it unusable in real applications. `instance_exec(&stored)` is not possible. -> D8 / D9 revised to the generated `case` statement approach |
| 3 | **Whether a tree-walking interpreter with a tagged union `Value` passes type inference, and whether rendering a 100-line template 10,000 times is fast enough** | **Result**: It passes. With a monomorphic `INode` AST + plain polymorphic values, 1.54 times the hand-written version and 3.2 times faster than CRuby. The `Value` wrapper was not adopted |
| 4 | Whether `first` is inferred as `Post \| nil` with a base `Relation` and `PostRelation`, without the types widening across several models | **Result**: It is inferred. Abstract stubs in the base, setters that return `nil`, and nullable attributes through cast methods are required |
| 5 | 100 concurrent connections with `TCPServer` + green threads + keep-alive, latency, whether issue #4528 reproduces | **Result**: 52-58k req/s at c=100 keep-alive (`SPINEL_WORKERS=1`), p99 ≤ 2 ms. Silent connections do not stop the others. However, always call `wait_readable(timeout)` immediately before `readpartial` (a bare `readpartial` occupies the OS worker and causes the same stall as #4528) |
| 6 | SQLite FFI open / prepare / bind / step / column, `blocking: true`, use of a `SizedQueue` pool from multiple threads, WAL | **Result**: Works. A race crash on the static `ffi_buffer` was avoided with `malloc` scratch space |
| 7 | FFI for `execv`, whether `trap` is available, `SO_REUSEADDR` | **Result**: All work (`trap("HUP")` confirmed on a real machine, an execv shim, immediate rebinding of the same port). D12 confirmed |
| 8 | HMAC with `openssl` / `digest`, `json`, `escapeHTML` from `cgi`, query parsing with `uri` | **Result**: json / uri / securerandom / base64 / strscan / stringio work. `cgi` does not exist in this tag, so HTML escaping is our own. HMAC is implemented by hand on top of `Digest::SHA256` |

go / no-go: settle D8 / D9 from the results of 2 and 3 before moving on to M1.

## 10. Milestones

- **M0** Spikes complete and the go / no-go decision.
- **M1** HTTP server, Request / Response, middleware, routing with a hand-written route table, static files, logging. A `hello world` controller works.
- **M2** Template engine, layouts, partials, helpers, `view_assigns` generation.
- **M3** SQLite adapter, migrations, `db/schema.rb` dump, model generation, validations, `Cybertrain::Test`.
- **M4** routes generation and URL helpers, form helpers, sessions, flash, CSRF. The blog works end to end and the integration tests pass in CI. **Acceptance criteria**.
- **M5** Development loop, error pages, `cybertrain new` / `generate scaffold`, README.

## 11. Repository layout

Framework (this repository):

```
cybertrain/
  spin.toml  cybertrain.rb  cybertrain.gemspec    # the gem holds only the CLI and what it requires
  cybertrain/
    main.rb  application.rb  config.rb  views.rb  version.rb   # entry point and boot for bin/<name>.rb
    db.rb  dev.rb  generator.rb  template.rb  schema.rb   # require entry points for each subdirectory
    router.rb  controller.rb  params.rb  session.rb  flash.rb  callback.rb  errors.rb  context.rb
    middleware.rb  app.rb   # Middleware base class and the default stack (App)
    http/        # server.rb  parser.rb  request.rb  response.rb  query.rb  cookies.rb
    middleware/  # request_logger  static  method_override  session_store  csrf_protection  error_pages
    template/    # lexer  parser  ast  inode  interpreter  helpers  form_builder  engine
    model.rb  relation.rb  validator.rb  cast.rb  migration.rb
    schema/      # table.rb  definition.rb  dumper.rb (the db/schema.rb DSL)
    db/          # sqlite_ffi.rb  connection.rb  pool.rb  migrator.rb  schema_dumper.rb  sqlite_ddl.rb  cli.rb  error.rb
    generator/   # runner.rb  routes_dsl.rb  *_emitter.rb  model_scan.rb  controller_scan.rb  manifest.rb  inflector.rb  url_support.rb
    dev/         # watcher.rb  rebuilder.rb  reexec.rb  error_page.rb (D12, section 6)
    cli.rb  cli/ # new_app.rb  scaffold.rb  templates.rb  build.rb
    test.rb  test/client.rb  html.rb  crypto.rb  logger.rb
  bin/cybertrain.rb  exe/cybertrain   # CLI (spin install / gem)
  script/regen-snapshot
  test/                     # spin test (.expected files are committed)
  examples/blog/            # the acceptance-criteria app
  spikes/NOTES.md           # record of the M0 spikes (the programs were deleted after verification; they are in the git history)
  README.md  docs/design.md  docs/deploy.md  docs/deploy.en.md  docs/deploy-kamal.md  docs/guide/template-language.md
  .github/workflows/ci.yml
```

App (generated by `cybertrain new blog`):

```
blog/
  spin.toml                 # [dependencies] cybertrain = ...
  spin.lock                 # written by spin lock (absent in examples/blog, which uses a path dependency)
  .gitignore  README.md
  app/controllers/  app/models/  app/views/  app/helpers/
  config/routes.rb  config/app.rb  config/deploy.yml   # deploy.yml: Kamal (D11)
  config/locales/en.yml     # the application's dictionary (D79)
  Dockerfile  .dockerignore  .kamal/secrets
  .github/workflows/ci.yml  # calls the setup-cybertrain action (D32)
  db/migrate/  db/schema.rb
  gen/                      # app.rb  controllers.rb  locales.rb  migrations.rb  models/  routes.rb  views.rb
  bin/blog.rb  bin/gen.rb  bin/db.rb   # blog.rb: server / migrate / db; db.rb: migrations during development
  test/                     # test_helper.rb, integration/health_test.rb, fixtures/, support/ (examples/blog: models/ controllers/ integration/ ... and fixtures/)
  public/  storage/  tmp/   # build/ and dist/ are in .gitignore; public/up.html is the Kamal health check
```

## 12. References

- Spinel: https://github.com/matz/spinel (README, `docs/limitations.md`, `docs/FFI.md`, `docs/spin.md`, `packages/`)
- Spinel issue #4528 (a thread stalled on a socket read stops other I/O): https://github.com/matz/spinel/issues/4528
- spin-index: https://github.com/matz/spin-index

## 13. Implementation history (2026-09-25 onwards)

Counts, timings and known gaps in each entry describe that entry's date; they are not a current test inventory or a fresh benchmark.

- Milestones M0-M5 in section 10 have all been reached. The acceptance criteria in section 2.2 are met by `examples/blog` (Article + Comment, server-side HTML, SQLite, a single binary with `spin build`); its 21 integration test cases and the framework's own 57 `spin test` programs pass.
- The development loop (D12) was confirmed on a real machine: view edits take effect immediately without a rebuild, and a controller edit was rebuilt in about 24 seconds (`spin run gen` + `spin build`; the build target at the time was the server bin) and replaced by the new binary via `execv`, keeping the same PID.
- The 42 Spinel constraints found during implementation are in `spikes/NOTES.md`. User-facing explanations are in `README.md` and `docs/guide/template-language.md`.
- Known gaps: the default value syntax for strict locals (`<%# locals: (comment: nil) %>`), `Model#attribute_or_method?` is always true (a mistyped attribute name renders as an empty string; closed 2026-11-11 by D71), `spin run db migrate` needs `--` (`spin run db -- migrate`), and under `SPINEL_GC_STRESS=1` the first `form_with` returns an empty string (suspected routing problem on the Spinel side).
- 2026-09-26: The application entry point was unified into `bin/<name>.rb` (`Cybertrain::Main`: server / migrate / db). `spin run gen` always writes `gen/views.rb`, and with `--embed-views` embeds `app/views/` as a string table. Production renders only from the embedded table (`Template::Engine.embedded`) and refuses to start if it is empty. Added `cybertrain migration` / `db` / `server` / `build`; `build` assembles `dist/` (the binary, public/, and storage/ and tmp/ if they do not exist). `bin/db.rb` is kept for migrations during development (`bin/<name>.rb` reads `gen/app`, so it cannot compile until the first migration creates `gen/models/`; `cybertrain migration` uses `spin run db -- migrate`, and production uses `dist/<name> migrate`). Cross builds and embedding public/ are out of scope.
- 2026-09-27 (up to PR #5): The CLI is also distributed as a gem (`gem install cybertrain`; `cybertrain new` writes the same tag as the dependency; D11). `cybertrain build` holds `tmp/cybertrain-build.lock` (containing the PID) until dist/ is assembled, and swaps in `dist/<name>` and `dist/public/` with rename (`.public.old`). The development server skips rebuilds while the lock is held and logs that it did, and removes and ignores locks with a dead PID or older than 30 minutes. (Revised 2026-11-13: the lock is now the kernel's, an exclusive `flock` on that file held by `cybertrain build` for the whole build (`CLI::BuildLock`). A second build that cannot have it exits 1; the kernel drops it when the holder exits or is killed, so there is no stale lock and no age rule, and a build of any length keeps it. The PID in the file is informational. The holder deletes the file on release while still holding the lock, and a taker that locked a file no longer at the path starts again. The development server asks for a shared `flock` to tell whether a build runs, and never deletes the file.) When the port cannot be bound, it prints `PortInUse` (in use / permission denied) to stdout and exits with code 1. `schema:dump` writes only where `db/` exists (exit code 1 otherwise). Added `script/regen-snapshot`. `spin test` has 64 programs.
- 2026-10-22: File uploads (D16). On the framework side: `Multipart` / `MultipartForm` / `UploadedFile` / `BlobStore`, `Params#upload`, `Request#multipart_form`, `Controller#send_blob`, `form_with(multipart: true)` / `f.file_field`, and 3 `Config` keys. On the CLI side: `cybertrain generate attachment` and scaffold's `FIELD:attachment`. The gem E2E in CI generates and compiles a scaffold with an attachment, runs the generated tests, and checks upload and download with curl (byte-identical content, headers).
- 2026-10-22: Authentication (D15). `cybertrain generate authentication` writes the users / sessions migrations, the `User` / `Session` models, `SessionsController` / `RegistrationsController`, views, an insertion into `ApplicationController`, routes, and `test/authentication.rb`. On the framework side: `Cybertrain::Password` (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256), `Controller.skip_before_action`, `Mapper#resource`, and a rule that leaves the derived names of custom routes unnamed when they collide. The gem E2E in CI compiles the generated application and runs sign-up / sign-in integration tests.
- 2026-10-23: `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` became the default on every response (D14, D16). `Response#to_http` emits them only when no header of the same name is present. Added webp / avif / woff / ttf / otf / wasm and others to `Static::EXTENSIONS`. No `Config` switch was added; override with `set_header`.
- 2026-10-25: Application-defined settings (D17, issue #69). They are declared with `Config#setting_string` / `setting_int` / `setting_bool` and read with `string_setting` / `int_setting` / `bool_setting`. `help` prints the list of declarations, and the startup banner prints the current values (secrets masked). The regression test is `test/config_settings.rb`.
- 2026-10-30: An idle keep-alive connection waits for its next request in one park instead of `ACCEPT_POLL` slices, and the drain ends idle connections on shutdown by shutting their read side (D14). On one CPU, 1,000 idle connections cost 8.6% of it before (docs/performance-next.md 8). The Dockerfile `cybertrain new` writes, and examples/blog's, set `SPINEL_GC_FULL_INTERVAL=8` (docs/spinel-feedback.md 5).
- 2026-11-05: Phase 1 of the Rails-gap programme (D1; [the design](superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-phase1-realtime-views-processes-design.md) and [the plan](superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-phase1-realtime-views-processes-plan.md)), in five packages merged together. ## 14. Proposed follow-ups from the 2026-11-11 review

These proposals require separate implementation decisions and tests. They do not change existing API behaviour, compiler workarounds or CI triggers.

- **Exact decimals (D42):** investigate BigDecimal throughout casting, generated models, relations, serialization and SQLite reads/writes. On the pinned compiler a standalone probe distinguishes `BigDecimal("9999999999.12345678")` from `BigDecimal("9999999999.12345679")`, rounds `BigDecimal("1.235")` to `BigDecimal("1.24")` at scale 2, and compares them correctly; converting the first two strings to Float makes them equal. This removes part of the original rejection rationale, not the need for integration testing. Define precision overflow, scale and rounding policy, and prove exact database round-trips: SQLite numeric affinity can lose precision even if Ruby uses BigDecimal. Do not migrate existing data or claim Rails decimal compatibility until storage and upgrade behaviour are decided.
- **Invalid input versus absent input (D42):** consider explicit cast/validation errors for malformed JSON, corrupt dates and non-finite decimal values. Today JSON can fall back to `{}`, a corrupt non-null date to 1970-01-01, and non-finite decimals pass through. Preserve a separately defined blank/null policy; any stricter default needs migration guidance and tests so existing applications are not silently broken.
- **Rails semantic compatibility (principle 4):** maintain a compatibility table for similarly named APIs, including return types, failure behaviour and migration examples. Start with decimal, `params.expect`, transactions and encryption. Document differences where users encounter the API, not only in historical decision notes.
- **Generator syntax (D4):** specify the supported declaration grammar and diagnose unsupported declarations with file/line context rather than silently losing metadata. The current controller scan can mistake `"@x = 1"` inside a string for an assignment and misses nested destructuring. Evaluate a tokenizer or parser against concrete failures and Spinel/build dependencies; retaining a lexical scan is an implementation choice, not a permanent architectural prohibition.
- **Evidence for CI reuse (D34):** before implementing the proposed merge-tree skip, require a successful check for the same source tree, toolchain and applicable workflow/configuration, with no missing or failed required checks. Tree equality alone is insufficient evidence. If successful equivalent validation cannot be established, run the checks. Keep this criterion with the D34 implementation proposal; the current CI is unchanged.
- **Document maintenance:** make current decisions authoritative at their original location and mark superseded decisions explicitly. Keep historical measurements and milestone records dated. For compiler restrictions, follow the [NOTES maintenance procedure](../spikes/NOTES.md#maintaining-this-inventory) and link to its per-version status rather than duplicating an unqualified claim; retire a workaround only with a test of the affected framework shape.
